# Fidelity register

Generated by `tools/fidelity_register.py`; edit the source annotations or declarations, then regenerate.

The acceptance gate is **zero unresolved assumptions in required original behavior**. All APPROX IDs below remain unresolved while their labels exist. Tags describing developer-only behavior or presentation conventions also remain listed; this inventory does not judge their relevance or downgrade them.

EXT labels identify deliberate extensions. DATA labels identify claimed original-data provenance. BIN labels cite the original executable logic a rule was traced to (`STP-PPC` = Mac PowerPC Sim Theme Park, `TPI-EXE` = Theme Park Inc Game.exe, `TPW-EXE` = Windows Sim Theme Park World v2.0 tp.exe; see docs/reverse/FINDINGS.md). None of these labels proves semantic correctness or cancels an APPROX label at the same site. Acceptance also requires evidence for required behavior, assets, inputs and outputs; a zero tag count alone cannot satisfy that gate.

This is a tag inventory, not enforcement of all semantics. It does not detect unlabelled magic numbers, formulas, logic, omitted features or false provenance claims. It scans whole-line C# // and /// comments under source, excluding bin/obj; inline trailing comments, block comments and other languages are outside its scope. ID templates and runtime log string templates are not code-site annotations.

```sh
python3 tools/fidelity_register.py --write
python3 tools/fidelity_register.py --check
python3 -m unittest discover -s tools -p 'test_fidelity_register.py' -v
```

Current inventory: **392 unresolved unique APPROX IDs**, 466 APPROX occurrences, 157 EXT occurrences, 170 DATA occurrences and 505 BIN occurrences.

Of these, 368 IDs belong to the existing original-fidelity areas (required-behavior scope remains unadjudicated), and 24 ONLINE IDs record uncertainty inside the allowed OpenTPW online extension. The extension scope does not hide, resolve or subtract those APPROX IDs from the total.

CI checks annotation/declaration consistency and document freshness only. It does not fail the build based on the unresolved count and does not establish the original-fidelity release gate.

Repeated source occurrences of one ID are allowed and listed separately. Duplicate register declarations, malformed/missing ID syntax, unregistered IDs and declarations without source annotations fail the check. Register declarations are read from the 22 configured C# registers (current one-entry-per-line tuple/new syntax) and the explicit three-column Approximation register table in docs/ONLINE.md; these are bounded lexical readers, not general C# or Markdown parsers. New registers must be explicitly configured. The check also compares the complete regenerated document against this file.

| ONLINE-009 | OpenTPW online extension | Edge and right-button steering add rate × offset × step per tick to the turn velocity. | The input branches of 0x005DC860. | `docs/ONLINE.md:350` |
| ONLINE-010 | OpenTPW online extension | The hovered city's name and park count are drawn at the top centre. | The layout of the original city labels 0x16c11/0x16c12. | `docs/ONLINE.md:351` |

| ONLINE-012 | OpenTPW online extension | Edge steering starts once the cursor has moved on the globe screen. | Whether the original steers from the first frame. | `docs/ONLINE.md:353` |

| ONLINE-016 | OpenTPW online extension | Each city-lobby island is the island of its park's theme. | The argument mapping of &#96;tp.exe&#96; 0x005E27B0 (what the top-10 list entry passes to 0x005E3210). | `docs/ONLINE.md:357` |

| ONLINE-018 | OpenTPW online extension | Each satellite's player count is drawn at its bottom-right corner. | The original wrote the count into the satellite control itself (STP-PPC 0x1b96d8), whose text placement is not decoded. | `docs/ONLINE.md:358` |
| ONLINE-019 | OpenTPW online extension | A chat park is shown as a flat overhead view in which each player is a marker that the server's relayed movement moves; the player walks their own marker by clicking (one walk speed; the original's double-click teleport is not separated). | The original chat park's three-dimensional scene, avatar model and animation (manual pp. 63-68) are not decoded. | `docs/ONLINE.md:359` |
| ONLINE-020 | OpenTPW online extension | The park details' eighth label row shows the players currently in the park's chat room (UITEXT 273). | The original's last row showed the Instant action mode flag (UITEXT 282), which the package format does not record. | `docs/ONLINE.md:361` |

| ONLINE-022 | OpenTPW online extension | The postcard photo attached by Send Postcard is the park's generated map thumbnail, and no park reference is attached. | The original attached a camcorder snapshot of the visited park (manual pp. 51/67); its content and encoding are not decoded. | `docs/ONLINE.md:362` |
| ONLINE-023 | OpenTPW online extension | Park names are masked with the word filter when published, and the stored masked name is what everyone sees. | The original showed swear words in park names as asterisks (online FAQ, docs/reverse/ONLINE-SOURCES.md S2); whether it filtered at entry or at display, and its word list, are not recovered (the list is the operator's &#96;swears.txt&#96;, ONLINE-001). | `docs/ONLINE.md:363` |
| ONLINE-024 | OpenTPW online extension | A buddy entering a park is reported as "Your buddy X has entered Y" from OpenTPW's own text. | The original notification's string; CHAT_COMMANDS 111-113 ("Your buddy ") have empty English suffixes and no enter-the-park variant is recovered. | `docs/ONLINE.md:364` |

## Retired and reserved IDs

IDs that a table row in docs keys, or docs prose cites as `APPROX:ID`, but no register declares: retired approximations and IDs a plan reserves. Declaring one of them fails the check, so a new approximation cannot take a number the docs already use with another meaning. `--write` keeps every listed ID; remove an entry by hand only when its docs agree with the new declaration.

| VM-901 | `source/OpenTPW/VM/Handlers/Scheduling.cs:23` | [APPROX:VM-901] The traced facts are STP-PPC 0xb0710–0xb0798: WAITABS stores now + operand as a 32-bit deadline word in the field WAIT uses (no script-speed division), its first encounter always yields, and later encounters wait while unsigned now &lt; deadline. The port models that deadline in its non-wrapping double script clock, so the original's unsigned comparison, its 2^32 wrap and its zero-deadline re-arm sentinel (docs/reverse/PPC-clock.md) are not modelled; a session that never nears 2^31 ms of scheduler clock cannot observe the difference. |

| STP-PPC:0x1003FC18 rating pass | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRating.cs:209` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x1003FC18 rating pass] start speed fWinchSpeed (+68), max speed and max height 0.0 (-13460(r2), 0x1003FCB8/BC); samples 1..n−2; trough coasters (flag 0x100) set sample flag 8 where the next segment climbs more than 0.01745 and clear it elsewhere (0x1003FDA4..0x1003FDC8); force a·(v/10) into sample +32..+40; speed into +28; the rating step reads the roll +24; lap += spacing / v; afterwards sample 0 gets speed fWinchSpeed and force 0, sample n−1 the speed and force of sample n−2 (0x1004001C..0x1004006C); then the suspended swing (flag 0x8), final (max Y, max speed) and the train count |

| STP-PPC:0x1003FE28 rating speed | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRating.cs:190` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x1003FE28 rating speed] acceleration = fAccelerationPerHeight × (y − y_next) (single), fused with fUphillAccelModifier (+40) when y_next &gt; y else fDownhillAccelModifier (+44); floored at fWinchSpeed (+68); × fFrictionMultiplier (+84) on sample flag 8; floored again; v == winch marks section flag 0x800; then min(v, controller +52) |
| STP-PPC:0x10040070 braking sample | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:194` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x10040070 braking sample] v_last = sample[(tail node +252) − 1].speed with the tail = controller +88 (the station end node); node +252 = trunc of the sampler's coordinate (last sample index + distance since it / spacing) when the walk reaches the node (0x10045790, 0x100457A0), i.e. the last sample before the node's knot |
| STP-PPC:0x10040070 braking start | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:214` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x10040070 braking start] controller +308 = max((n − 1) − v_last / 3.0, 0) samples |

| STP-PPC:0x10040650 coaster tick | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:478` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x10040650 coaster tick] hops of half a sample: per car the winch test reads the section of the sample at trunc(s + ½) (0x1004098C, 0x10040A0C), the average car speed, braking when a car is past ctl+308 (strictly) or friction on the flag-8 sample of the reference car's +64 position, which is written only after the frame's hops (0x10041094), so it is fixed per frame; max(·, W) when any car is on a winch section else max(·, M), min(·, ctl+52); τ = d/((v + v')/2) from the reference car's distance to its half-sample point (0x1004316C); a full hop if τ &lt; budget, else α = budget/τ, v_end = (1 − α)v + αv', s += ½·((v + v_end)/2·budget/d), v = min(cap, v_end) with no floor: 0x10040D98 stores max(v_end, M), but 0x10040D9C..0x10040DC8 reload the unfloored v_end and overwrite that store; after every hop v ×= 0.95 below 2.0 and v = 0 below 0.01; s wraps by fmod |

| STP-PPC:0x10040A20 coaster hop speed | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:466` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x10040A20 coaster hop speed] per car: v_c = max(fmadds(mod, PerHeight × (y(s) − y(s + ½)), v), floor) with fUphillAccelModifier when y(s + ½) &gt; y(s), else fDownhillAccelModifier; the floor is fWinchSpeed·P on winch (0x800) sections, else fMinSpeed·P |

| STP-PPC:0x100415B4 rating step | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRating.cs:16` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x100415B4 rating step] definition +812 GForceSickness × \|F\|₁, +816 UpsideDownSickness × u, +820 Speed × v, +824 GForceExcitement × \|F\|₁, +832 BankExcitement × \|bank\|, +836 XGForceUncomfortable, +840 InvertedUncomfortable, +844/+848 scream start deltas, +852/+856 scream sustain excitements |
| STP-PPC:0x100415B4 rating step | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRating.cs:86` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x100415B4 rating step] \|F\|₁ = (\|Fx\| + \|Fy\|) + \|Fz\|; U = max(\|bank\| − π/2, 0), u = U + (U ≠ 0); separate single multiplies and adds; inverted discomfort u × InvertedUncomfortable when u ≠ 0 and Fy &lt; 0.1, tripled when Fy &lt; 0, otherwise the f0 the call site 0x1003FFA8 leaves live: Fz (sample +40, ORACLE F7, 509/509 bit-exact); S and E decay by 0.5; screams after sample 2 (flags & ~7, then 4 normal, 2 terror) |
| STP-PPC:0x100417BC rating decay | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRating.cs:108` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x100417BC rating decay] S = fmadds(S, 0.5, dS) and E = fmadds(E, 0.5, dE) (0x100417BC, 0x100417CC); the peaks are fcmpo selects that keep the old peak on NaN |

| STP-PPC:0x10041B00 segment curve | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCurve.cs:114` | [BIN:STP-PPC:0x10041B00 segment curve] Q(t) = P0 + t(3P1 − 3P0) + t²(3P0 − 6P1 + 3P2) + t³(−P0 + 3P1 − 3P2 + P3), fused single operations |

Up to depth 128 the cell's own link to the front picks the axis: link 1 (−Y) gives (lateral, depth), 4 (+X) (255 − depth, lateral), 16 (+Y) (lateral, 255 − depth), 64 (−X) (depth, lateral). Beyond 128 (the fourth place) the axis is the link of the queue cell behind (0x004DE670), for the back cell the direction from its attached path cell (the first of its connections 1, 4, 16, 64 that leads to a path cell, reversed), and link 4 then gives depth − 128 instead of 255 − depth |
